 THIS WRIT PETITION IS FILED UNDER ARTICLES 226 AND 227
OF THE CONSTITUTION OF INDIA, PRAYING TO DIRECT THE
RESPONDENTS TO INCLUDE THE NAMES OF THESE PETITIONERS IN
THE FINAL ELIGIBLE VOTERS LIST AND PERMIT THE PETITIONERS
TO CAST THEIR VOTES IN THE UPCOMING ELECTIONS OF THE
RESPONDENT NO.3 SOCIETY WHICH IS SCHEDULED TO BE HELD ON
04.02.2025.
 THESE WRIT PETITIONS COMING ON FOR ORDERS, THIS DAY,
ORDER WAS MADE THEREIN AS UNDER:
CORAM: HON'BLE MRS JUSTICE K.S. HEMALEKHA

 - 383 -
NC: 2025:KHC:9252
WP No. 2724 of 2025
C/W WP No. 3029 of 2025
WP No. 3043 of 2025
AND 2 OTHER
ORAL ORDER
 Petitioners are seeking for a direction to the
respondents to include the names of the petitioners in the
final eligible voters list and permit the petitioners to cast
their votes in the elections of respondent No.3-society,
which is scheduled to be held on 04.02.2025.
2. This Court by way of an interim arrangement
permitted the petitioners to cast their votes in the ensuing
election of respondent No.3-society scheduled on
04.02.2025 subject to final outcome of this petition.
3. Heard Sri Dhananjay Kumar and Sri Rakshith
Kumar, learned counsel for the petitioners, Sri Yogesh D.
Naik, learned AGA for respondent Nos.1 and 4, Sri T.L.
Kiran Kumar, learned counsel for respondent No.2 and Sri
Kethan Kumar, learned counsel for respondent No.3.
4. Reiterating various contentions raised in the
writ petition, it is mainly urged that no individual notice is
issued to the petitioners while the names of the petitioners

 - 384 -
NC: 2025:KHC:9252
WP No. 2724 of 2025
C/W WP No. 3029 of 2025
WP No. 3043 of 2025
AND 2 OTHER
are excluded in the final voters list and the respondents
have acted against mandate requirement of Rule 13 (D)
(2-A) of the Karnataka Co-Operative Societies Rules, 1960
(‘the Rules’ for short). It is urged that the petitioners
should be treated as eligible voters and their votes to be
counted as valid votes. The petitioners state that they
were not permitted to vote in compliance with the order
passed by this Court.
5. The Returning Officer is present before the
Court. It is brought to the notice of this Court by the
learned AGA that out of the total petitioners who have
approached this Court, only 526 petitioners have cast
votes in the election of respondent No.3-society that was
scheduled on 04.02.2025 and filed an affidavit of the
Returning Officer indicating at paragraph Nos.13 and 14 as
under:
 “13. I humbly submit that one Fathima Zuhara is no
a member of the Society as her membership was

 - 385 -
NC: 2025:KHC:9252
WP No. 2724 of 2025
C/W WP No. 3029 of 2025
WP No. 3043 of 2025
AND 2 OTHER
closed on 18.03.2017 the same is herewith produced
and marked as DOCUMENT NO.1.
14. One Naseema, Membership No.4400 also cast
her vote. One Narayana, Ayeesha Banu, Evalyn Lobo
Jante those who have not mentioned their member
number in the petition which was very difficult to find
out their names in the list on the spot.”
6. Learned counsel for respondent No.3-society
fairly submits that the society has not followed the
mandatory requirement of the aforementioned Rules and
cast votes of the petitioners to be included while counting
and the results to be announced.
7. It is brought to the notice of this Court that
under identical circumstances, the Co-Ordinate Bench of
this Court in the case of H.S. Raju and others Vs. State
of Karnataka and others1
(H.S. Raju) has permitted the
votes to be counted and election results to be
announced, keeping open all contentions to be
adjudicated in the appropriate forum under Section 70

1
 2022 (4) AKR 775

 - 386 -
NC: 2025:KHC:9252
WP No. 2724 of 2025
C/W WP No. 3029 of 2025
WP No. 3043 of 2025
AND 2 OTHER
(2) (c) of the Karnataka Co-Operative Societies Act, 1959
(‘the Act’ for short). Whether the petitioners would incur
any disqualification to vote or not is a question of fact to
be decided by the appropriate authority under Section 70
(2) (c) of the Act and hence, the following:
ORDER
i. Writ petitions are disposed of.
ii. The Returning Officer to count the votes,
including the votes cast by petitioners and
announce the results of the election, which
was scheduled on 04.02.2025.
iii. Aggrieved party is entitled to raise a
dispute under Section 70 (2) (c) of the Act,
if raised a dispute under the said provision,
questioning the eligibility of the petitioners
to vote in the election to the Board of the
respondent – society, the said dispute shall
be decided without being influenced by the
interim order granted by this Court and it is
made clear that granting of an interim
order in favour of the petitioner would not
give any finding on the eligibility of the

 - 387 -
NC: 2025:KHC:9252
WP No. 2724 of 2025
C/W WP No. 3029 of 2025
WP No. 3043 of 2025
AND 2 OTHER
petitioners to vote in the election of the
Board of respondent – society.
iv. The eligibility of the petitioners to vote in
the election is kept open to be decided in a
dispute, if any, raised in this regard.
v. If any such dispute relating to the eligibility
of the petitioners to vote in the election to
the Board of the respondent-society is
raised, the effect of holding the petitioners
ineligible to vote and excluding such votes,
on the final outcome of the election results
shall also be taken into account before
passing the final orders.
vi. The vote cast by the petitioners shall be
kept in a separate sealed box after
counting.
vii. This Court has not expressed any merits or
demerits of the ineligibility of the
petitioners.
 Sd/-

 JUSTICE K.S. HEMALEKHA
